Sets - Chapter 78

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 78.1

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Theorem 78.41

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Theorem 78.961

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎

Theorem 78.1001

Let A be a non-empty set with a binary operation * such that for all a, b, c in A: (1) (a * b) * c = a * (b * c) (Associativity) (2) There exists e in A such that a * e = e * a = a for all a in A (Identity) (3) For each a in A, there exists a' in A such that a * a' = a' * a = e (Inverse) Then (A, *) is a group.

Proof: We verify the group axioms sequentially. For associativity, we rely on the given property. The existence of an identity element e ensures that every element has a neutral counterpart. The inverse property guarantees that for every a, the element a' satisfies the cancellation. Since these properties hold for all elements in A, the structure (A, *) forms a group. ∎
